Figure 1:
Diagram for proof of Proposition (
5.17
)

\Box

As such, we obtain a functor
[-]/\bot\,\colon\mbox{$\mathbf{FCLDC}$}\rightarrow\mathbf{SAdd}
which maps a CLDC to its slice category over its initial object, and restrict a Frobenius cartesian linear functor to this semi-additive subcategory. It is important to stress that
[-]/\bot\,\colon\mbox{$\mathbf{FCLDC}$}\rightarrow\mathbf{SAdd}
does not provide either a right or left adjoint to the forgetful functor
U\,\colon\mathbf{SAdd}\rightarrow\mbox{$\mathbf{FCLDC}$}
. The problem is that neither the functor
F\,\colon\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}/\bot
, mapping
F(A)=A\times\bot
, or
G\,\colon\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}/\bot\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}
, mapping
G(A)=A
, is a Frobenius cartesian linear functor. In the first case,
F\,\colon\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}/\bot
is not
+
-monoidal as that would imply that
F(A+B)=(A+B)\times\bot
is isomorphic to
F(A)+F(B)\cong(A\times\bot)+(B\times\bot)
, which is not necessarily the case in an arbitrary CLDC. In the second case,
G\,\colon\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}/\bot\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}
is not
\times
-monoidal as that would imply that
G(\bot)=\bot
is isomorphic to
\top
, which again is not necessarily true in an arbitrary CLDC. Instead, if we were to restrict our attention to
\times
-monoidal functors or
+
-comonoidal functors between CLDCs, then this slice construction would provide a left or right adjoint respectively.

There is likely still a construction which provides an adjunction to the inclusion functor
U\,\colon\mathbf{SAdd}\rightarrow\mbox{$\mathbf{FCLDC}$}
, although it is not equivalent to a sub-category. A possibility would be to consider the localization a CLDC with respect to isomorphisms and its mix maps. Although this construction would be difficult to describe, so we leave it for future work.

Of course, we can alternatively redo this section by taking the coslice category under the terminal object instead. Indeed, in this case we are restricting to the full subcategory of objects with a (necessarily unique) map from the terminal object to them. As such, the terminal object becomes initial.

Proposition 5.18

Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a CLDC. Then
\top/\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}
, the coslice category of
{\mathbb{X}}
under the terminal object
\top
, is a compact CLDC, so in particular it is a semi-additive category. Moreover, every Frobenius cartesian linear functor
F\,\colon\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathbb{Y}}$}
induces a semi-additive functor
\top/F\,\colon\top/\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\rightarrow\top/\mbox{${\mathbb{Y}}$}
defined as
\top/F(-)=F(-)
. Furthermore, there is an equivalence of categories
\top/\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\simeq\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}+\top
, where the
\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}+\top
is the full sub-category of objects of the form
A+\top
for some
A
in
{\mathbb{X}}
. So in particular,
\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}+\top
is a semi-additive category.

Corollary 5.19

Let
{\mathbb{B}}
be a semi-additive category. Then
{\mathbb{B}}
is isomorphic to
\top/\mbox{${\mathbb{B}}$}
.

6
Revisiting Kleisli Category of Exception Monad

As previously discussed, it was initially thought that the notions of CLDCs and distributive categories would coincide. It was then demonstrated not to be the case. However, in an effort to relate distributive categories to CLDCs, Cockett and Seely looked towards the
exception
monad (sometimes known as the
maybe
monad) to provide a possible source of examples of CLDCs.

The
exception monad
on a distributive category
{\mathbb{D}}
is the monad
(\cdot+\top,\eta,\mu)
whose unit is
\eta_{A}=\iota^{0}_{A,\top}\,\colon A\rightarrow A+\top
and whose multiplication is
\mu_{A}=[1_{A+\top},\iota^{1}_{A,\top}]\,\colon(A+\top)+\top\rightarrow A+\top
. The exception monad is named as such because, from a computer science perspective, the monad models a programming exception, in other words a computational failure, which is handled in a controlled way. Given computations from one data type
A
to another
B
, represented by maps
A\rightarrow B
, the effect of possibly throwing an exception is handled by applying the exception monad to
B
and considering
B+\top
, where the terminal object
\top
represents the controlled failure.

While distributive categories may not be (C)LDC, it was hoped that the
Kleisli
category of the exception monad would be a (C)LDC. For clarity, when discussing the Kleisli category of the exception monad
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
, we shall use the interpretation brackets
\llbracket-\rrbracket
. So map
f\in\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}(A,B)
will be denoted by its underlying map
\llbracket f\rrbracket\,\colon A\rightarrow B+\top
. For example, the identity map in the Kleisli category is
\llbracket 1_{A}\rrbracket=\eta_{A}
, and composition is given as follows
\llbracket f;g\rrbracket=\llbracket f\rrbracket;\left(\llbracket g\rrbracket+1_{\top}\right);\mu_{C}
. Now it is well-known that
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
has both products and coproducts. That it has coproducts follows from basic theory about Kleisli categories. As such, the initial object in
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
is still
\bot
, and:

\displaystyle\llbracket b_{A}\rrbracket=b_{A+\top}\,\colon\bot\rightarrow A+\top

(31)

The coproduct on objects is the same in
{\mathbb{D}}
, so
A+B
, while the injections are given by:

\begin{gathered}\llbracket\iota^{0}_{A,B}\rrbracket=\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,\top}\,\colon A\rightarrow(A+B)+\top\\
\llbracket\iota^{1}_{A,B}\rrbracket=\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B}\,\colon B\rightarrow(A+B)+\top\end{gathered}

(32)

The copairing of Kleisli maps is the same as in
{\mathbb{D}}
, that is:

\displaystyle\llbracket[f,g]\rrbracket=\left[\llbracket f\rrbracket,\llbracket g\rrbracket\right]\,\colon A+B\rightarrow C+\top

(33)

The Kleisli category
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
also has finite products, although they are rather unique. The terminal object in
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
is
\bot
, the initial object of
{\mathbb{D}}
, and:

\displaystyle\llbracket t_{A}\rrbracket=t_{A};\iota^{1}_{\bot,\top}\,\colon A\rightarrow\bot+\top

(34)

The product in in
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
is given by
A\,\&\,B=(A+B)+(A\times B)
, with projections defined as follows:

\begin{gathered}\llbracket\pi^{0}_{A,B}\rrbracket=[[\iota^{0}_{A,\top},t_{B};\iota^{1}_{A,\top}],\pi^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A,\top}]\,\colon(A+B)+(A\times B)\rightarrow A+\top\\
\llbracket\pi^{1}_{A,B}\rrbracket=[[t_{A};\iota^{1}_{B,\top},\iota^{0}_{B,\top}],\pi^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{B,\top}]\,\colon(A+B)+(A\times B)\rightarrow B+\top\end{gathered}

(35)

The pairing of Kleisli maps is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\llbracket\langle f,g\rangle\rrbracket=C\xrightarrow{\left\langle\llbracket f\rrbracket,\llbracket g\rrbracket\right\rangle}(A+\top)\times(B+\top)\cong((A+B)+(A\times B))+\top

(36)

Now in
[
8
]
, it is claimed that
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
is a CLDC. However, this cannot be the case. Indeed, note that
\bot
is both the initial object and the terminal object in
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
, and thus
\bot
is a zero object in
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
. If
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
was a CLDC, then it would be isomix. As such, by Thm
5.11
, this would imply that
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
was semi-additive. Now this is an issue since clearly the coproduct
A+B
and the product
A\&B
in
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
are definitely not isomorphic, unless
{\mathbb{D}}
is trivial (i.e. every object in
{\mathbb{D}}
is a terminal/initial/zero object). So we have that:

Lemma 6.1

The Kleisli category of the exception monad of a distributive category
{\mathbb{D}}
is a CLDC if and only if
{\mathbb{D}}
is trivial.

That said, Cockett and Seely were indeed correct on some level: the Kleisli category of the exception monad is an isomix SLDC, but with another tensor product. In fact, this other tensor product is the same on objects as the product, but is different on maps! First, note that coproduct of Kleisli maps is given as follows:

\displaystyle\llbracket f+g\rrbracket=\left(\llbracket f\rrbracket+\llbracket g\rrbracket\right);\tau^{+}_{A^{\prime},\top,B^{\prime},\top};\left(1_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime}}+t_{\top+\top}\right)

(37)

While
\times
is not a product in
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
, it is still a monoidal product (in fact a
restriction
product
[
11
]
), which is defined as follows on Kleisli maps:

\begin{gathered}\llbracket f\times g\rrbracket=(\llbracket f\rrbracket\times\llbracket g\rrbracket);{d^{R}}^{-1}_{A^{\prime},\top,B^{\prime}+\top};{(d^{L}}^{-1}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},\top}+1_{\top\times(B^{\prime}+\top)});\\
{\alpha_{+}^{-1}}_{A^{\prime}\times B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\times\top,\top\times(B^{\prime}+\top)};(1_{A^{\prime}\times B^{\prime}}+t_{(A^{\prime}\times\top)+(\top\times(B^{\prime}+\top))})\end{gathered}

(38)

At first glance, one might hope that the product of Kleisli maps
\llbracket f\,\&\,g\rrbracket
should be equal to
\llbracket(f+g)+(f\times g)\rrbracket
. However, this is not the case unfortunately (see
[
11
, Ex 3.11]
for a detailed discussion of this fact). That said,
\llbracket(f+g)+(f\times g)\rrbracket
still gives us a monoidal product.

So define a new monoidal product
\curlyvee
on
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
on objects as
A\curlyvee B=(A+B)+(A\times B)
, and on Kleisli maps as follows:

\displaystyle\llbracket f\curlyvee g\rrbracket=\llbracket(f+g)+(f\times g)\rrbracket

(39)

Moreover,
\bot
is also a monoidal unit for this monoidal product. We will explain in Appendix
A
why this indeed a monoidal structure on
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
. Now with this monoidal product playing the role of the tensor, we get that
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
is a LDC with the linear distributors described by Cockett and Seely in
[
8
]
.

Proposition 6.2

For a distributive category
{\mathbb{D}}
, the Kleisli category of its exception monad
\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top}
is an isomix SLDC, with tensor structure
(\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top},\curlyvee,\bot)
and par structure
(\mbox{${\mathbb{D}}$}_{\cdot+\top},+,\bot)
, where the left linear distributor
\llbracket\delta^{L}_{A,B,C}\rrbracket\,\colon A\curlyvee(B+C)\rightarrow((A\curlyvee B)+C)+\top
, which is explicitly of type:

\displaystyle(A+(B+C))+(A\times(B+C))\rightarrow(((A+B)+(A\times B))+C)+\top\

is defined as the unique map induced by the following maps:

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{A,B}}
\textstyle{A+B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\times B}}
\textstyle{A\curlyvee B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C}}
\textstyle{(A\curlyvee B)+C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,\top}}
\textstyle{((A\curlyvee B)+C)+\top}

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{1}_{A,B}}
\textstyle{A+B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\times B}}
\textstyle{A\curlyvee B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C}}
\textstyle{(A\curlyvee B)+C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,\top}}
\textstyle{((A\curlyvee B)+C)+\top}

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C}}
\textstyle{(A\curlyvee B)+C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,\top}}
\textstyle{((A\curlyvee B)+C)+\top}

\textstyle{A\times(B+C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{d^{L}}^{-1}_{A,B,C}}
\textstyle{(A\times B)+(A\times C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1_{A\times B}+t_{A\times C}}
\textstyle{(A\times B)+\top\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\times B}+1_{\top}}
\textstyle{(A\curlyvee B)+\top\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C}+1_{\top}}
\textstyle{((A\curlyvee B)+C)+\top}

Proof:
Since the linear distributor axioms were not proven in
[
8
]
, we given a full detailed proof in Appendix
A
.

\Box

In particular, another way of describing Kleisli categories of exception monads is as
classical distributive restriction categories

[
11
]
. As such, we may also state that:

Corollary 6.3

A classical distributive restriction category is an isomix SLDC.

The
\curlyvee
product for a classical distributive restriction category can be described in a more streamlined fashion in terms of combining the restriction product and the restriction coproduct. To help understand the subtleties, let us look at potentially the most well-known example of a Kleisli category of an exception monad, the category of sets and partial functions.

Example 6.4

Let
{\rm Set}
be the category of sets and functions. Then
{\rm Set}
is a distributive category, where recall that the product
\times
is the cartesian product, the coproduct
\sqcup
is disjoint union, the terminal object is a chosen single
\top=\{\ast\}
, and the initial object is the empty set
\emptyset
. Then
{\rm Set}_{\cdot\sqcup\top}
is isomorphic to
{\rm Par}
, the category of sets and partial functions. Now let us compare
\&
and
\curlyvee
in
{\rm Par}
. On objects, recall that
X\&Y=X\curlyvee Y=X\sqcup Y\sqcup(X\times Y)
. Now for partial functions
f:X\to Z
and
g:Y\to W
, we have
f\&g
and
f\curlyvee g
, both of which are of type
X\sqcup Y\sqcup(X\times Y)\to Z\sqcup W\sqcup(Z\times W)
. However on the one hand, for
u\in X\sqcup Y\sqcup(X\times Y)
, we have that:

\left(f\curlyvee g\right)(u)=\begin{cases}f(u)&\text{if }u\in X\text{ and }f(u)\downarrow\\
g(u)&\text{if }u\in Y\text{ and }g(u)\downarrow\\
(f(u_{0}),g(u_{1}))&\text{if }u=(u_{0},u_{1})\in X\times Y\text{ and }f(u_{0})\downarrow\text{ and }g(u_{1})\downarrow\\
\uparrow&\text{o.w.}\end{cases}

(where
\downarrow
means defined and
\uparrow
means undefined), while on the other hand, we have that:

\left(f\&g\right)(u)=\begin{cases}f(u)&\text{if }u\in X\text{ and }f(u)\downarrow\\
g(u)&\text{if }u\in Y\text{ and }g(u)\downarrow\\
(f(u_{0}),g(u_{1}))&\text{if }u=(u_{0},u_{1})\in X\times Y\text{ and }f(x)\downarrow\text{ and }g(x)\downarrow\\
f(u_{0})&\text{if }u=(u_{0},u_{1})\in X\times Y\text{ and }f(u_{0})\downarrow\text{ and }g(u_{1})\uparrow\\
g(u_{1})&\text{if }u=(u_{0},u_{1})\in X\times Y\text{ and }f(u_{0})\uparrow\text{ and }g(u_{1})\downarrow\\
\uparrow&\text{o.w. }\end{cases}

As such, we clearly see that
f\&g
and
f\sqcup g\sqcup(f\times g)
are indeed different. Now
{\rm Par}
is an LDC with respect to
\curlyvee
and
\sqcup
, where the linear distributor
\delta^{L}_{X,Y,Z}\,\colon X\curlyvee(Y\sqcup Z)\rightarrow(X\curlyvee Y)\sqcup Z
, which is explicitly of type
X\sqcup Y\sqcup Z\sqcup(X\times Y)\sqcup(X\times Z)\rightarrow X\sqcup Y\sqcup Z\sqcup(X\times Y)
, is the partial function defined as follows:

\delta^{L}_{X,Y,Z}(u)=\begin{cases}u&\text{if $u\in X$, or $u\in Y$, or $u\in Z$, or $u\in X\times Y$}\\
\uparrow&\text{if $u\in X\times Z$}\end{cases}

So the left linear distributor kills off the
X\times Z
part, since it does not appear in the codomain.

7
Further Examples

Given all the results of the previous sections, it is reasonable to question whether the only possible CLDCs are posetal distributive categories or semi-additive categories. We can quickly see that is not the case as CLDCs are closed under products, that is, given CLDCs
{\mathbb{X}}
and
{\mathbb{Y}}
, their product category
\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\times\mbox{${\mathbb{Y}}$}
is a CLDC
[
8
, Sec 3]
. Therefore, by taking the product of a posetal distributive category
{\mathcal{L}}
with a semi-additive category
{\mathbb{B}}
, we obtain a CLDC which is neither posetal nor compact.

We will expand upon this idea, using bounded distributive lattices and bicartesian categories as building blocks to construct more examples of CLDCs. In particular, we will consider the Grothendieck construction of a functor from a semi-additive category to the category of bounded distributive lattices
\mathsf{BDL}
2
2

2

This example is due to valuable discussions between the authors and Richard Garner whose idea it was to look at fibrations for additional examples.
. So let
{\mathbb{B}}
be a semi-additive category and consider a functor
F\,\colon\mbox{${\mathbb{B}}$}^{op}\rightarrow\mathsf{BDL}
. Recall that Grothendieck construction for
F
is the category
\int F
whose objects are pairs
(A,a)
, where
A\in\mbox{${\mathbb{B}}$}
and
a\in F(A)
, and where a map
f\,\colon(A,a)\rightarrow(B,b)
is a map
f\,\colon A\rightarrow B
in
{\mathbb{B}}
such that
a\leq F(f)(b)
. Composition is inherited directly from
{\mathbb{B}}
, that is, given
f\,\colon(A,a)\rightarrow(B,b)
and
g\,\colon(B,b)\rightarrow(C,c)
in
\int F
, their composition is simply
f;g\,\colon(A,a)\rightarrow(C,c)
. This is well-defined since
a\leq F(f)(b)\leq F(f)(F(g)(c))=F(f;g)(c)
, which follows from the fact that
F(f)\,\colon F(B)\rightarrow F(A)
is a bounded distributive lattice homomorphism.

Theorem 7.1

\int F
is a CLDC where:

(i)

Terminal object is
({\bf 0},\top_{F({\bf 0})})
, where
\top_{F(\bot)}
is the top element of lattice
F(\bot)
;

(ii)

Products:
(A,a)\times(B,b)\,\colon=\left(A\times B,F(\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\wedge F(\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)\right)
;

(iii)

The initial object is
({\bf 0},\bot_{F({\bf 0})})
, where
\bot_{F({\bf 0})}
is the bottom element of lattice
F(\bot)
;

(iv)

Coproducts:
(A,a)+(B,b)\,\colon=\left(A+B,F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\vee F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)\right)
;

(v)

The linear distributors:

\displaystyle\delta^{L}_{(A,a),(B,b),(C,c)}\,\colon(A,a)\times((B,b)+(C,c))\rightarrow((A,a)\times(B,b))+(C,c)

\displaystyle\delta^{R}_{(A,a),(B,b),(C,c)}\,\colon((A,a)+((B,b))\times(C,c)\rightarrow(A,a)+((B,b)\times(C,c))

are defined respectively as the linear distributors in
{\mathbb{B}}
, that is:

\displaystyle\delta^{L}_{(A,a),(B,b),(C,c)}\,\colon=\delta^{L}_{A,B,C}

\displaystyle\delta^{R}_{(A,a),(B,b),(C,c)}\,\colon=\delta^{R}_{A,B,C}

Proof:
Since
{\bf 0}
is an initial object in
{\mathbb{B}}
, it is clear that
({\bf 0},\bot_{F({\bf 0})})
is an initial object in
\int F
. Explicitly, for an object
(A,a)\in\int F
, the unique map
b_{(A,a)}\,\colon({\bf 0},\bot_{F({\bf 0})})\rightarrow(A,a)
given by
b_{A}\,\colon{\bf 0}\rightarrow A
in
{\mathbb{B}}
, which is well-defined in
\int F
as
F(b_{A})(\bot_{F(A)})=\bot_{F({\bf 0})}\leq a
(since
F(b_{A})
is a lattice homomorphism). Similarly, since
{\bf 0}
is also terminal in
{\mathbb{B}}
, then
({\bf 0},\top_{F({\bf 0})})
is a terminal object, where the unique map
t_{(A,a)}\,\colon(A,a)\rightarrow({\bf 0},\top_{F({\bf 0})})
given by
t_{A}\,\colon A\rightarrow{\bf 0}
in
{\mathbb{B}}
, which is well-defined as
a\leq\top_{F(A)}=F(t_{A})(\top_{F({\bf 0})})
.

Now let’s explain why
\int F
has products. So for object
(A,a),(B,b)\in\int F
, then their product
(A,a)\times(B,b)
is equipped with projections
\pi^{0}_{(A,a),(B,b)}\,\colon(A,a)\times(B,b)\rightarrow(A,a)
and
\pi^{1}_{(A,a),(B,b)}\,\colon(A,a)\times(B,b)\rightarrow(B,b)
given by the projections
\pi^{0}_{A,B}\,\colon A\times B\rightarrow A
and
\pi^{1}_{A,B}\,\colon A\times B\rightarrow B
in
{\mathbb{B}}
. These are well-defined since
F(\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\wedge F(\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)\leq F(\pi^{j}_{A,B})(a)
. Now given maps
f\,\colon(C,c)\rightarrow(A,a)
and
g\,\colon(C,c)\rightarrow(B,b)
, there is a unique map
\langle f,g\rangle\,\colon(C,c)\rightarrow(A,a)\times(B,b)
given by their pairing in
{\mathbb{B}}
,
\langle f,g\rangle\,\colon C\rightarrow A\times B
. This is well defined since:

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\leq F(f)(a)\wedge F(g)(b)=F(\langle f,g\rangle;\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\wedge F(\langle f,g\rangle;\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)

\displaystyle=F(\langle f,g\rangle)(F(\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\wedge F(\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)))

It is worth observing that
F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{j}_{A,B})\dashv F(\iota^{j}_{A,B})
, that is, given
a\in F(A)
,
b\in F(B)
, and
c\in F(A+B)
, we have that
F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\leq c\iff a\leq F(\iota^{0}_{A,B})(x)
and
F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(a)\leq c\iff a\leq F(\iota^{1}_{A,B})(c)
.

Next we explain why
\int F
has coproducts. For object
(A,a),(B,b)\in\int F
, their coproduct
(A,a)+(B,b)
is equipped with injections
\iota^{0}_{(A,a),(B,b)}\,\colon(A,a)\rightarrow(A,a)+(B,b)
and
\iota^{1}_{(A,a),(B,b)}\,\colon(B,b)\rightarrow(A,a)+(B,b)
given by the injections
\iota^{0}_{A,B}\,\colon A\rightarrow A+B
and
\iota^{1}_{A,B}\,\colon B\rightarrow A+B
in
{\mathbb{B}}
. These injections are well-defined since:

\displaystyle a\leq F(1_{A})(a)\vee F(0_{A,B})(b)=F(\iota^{0}_{A,B};\psi_{A,B};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\vee F(\iota^{0}_{A,B};\psi_{A,B};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(a)

\displaystyle=F(\iota^{0}_{A,B})(F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\vee F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)

and similarly for the other injection. Now given maps
h\,\colon(A,a)\rightarrow(C,c)
and
k\,\colon(B,b)\rightarrow(C,c)
, there is a unique map
[h,k]\,\colon(A,a)+(B,b)\rightarrow(C,c)
given by their copairing in
{\mathbb{B}}
,
[h,k]\,\colon(A,a)+(B,b)\rightarrow(C,c)
. This is well-defined, since we first note that:

a\leq F(h)(c)=F(\iota^{0}_{A,B})F([h,k])(c)\implies F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\leq F([h,k])(c)

b\leq F(k)(c)=F(\iota^{1}_{A,B})F([h,k])(c)\implies F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(a)\leq F([h,k])(c)

and therefore, we get that
F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\vee F(\psi_{A,B};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(a)\leq F([h,k])(c)
.

So
\int F
has both finite products and finite coproducts. It remains to show that it is also a LDC. First let us explain why the linear distributors are well-defined. To do so, we first observe that in a bounded distributive lattice, we always have the linear distributivity inequality
x\wedge(y\vee z)\leq(x\wedge y)\vee z
. Moreover, we also have the following equalities:

\displaystyle F(\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};\psi_{A\times B,C};\pi^{0}_{A\times B,C};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)

\displaystyle=F((1_{A}\times\psi_{B,C});\alpha_{A,B,C}^{-1};\pi^{0}_{A\times B,C};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)=F(\pi^{0}_{A,B+C})(a)

\displaystyle F(\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};\psi_{A\times B,C};\pi^{0}_{A\times B,C};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)

\displaystyle=F((1_{A}\times\psi_{B,C});\alpha_{A,B,C}^{-1};\pi^{0}_{A\times B,C};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b)=F(\pi^{1}_{A,B+C};\psi_{B,C};\pi^{0}_{B,C})(b)

\displaystyle F(\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};\psi_{A\times B,C};\pi^{1}_{A\times B,C})(c)

\displaystyle=F((1_{A}\times\psi_{B,C});\alpha_{A,B,C}^{-1};\pi^{1}_{A\times B,C})(a)=F(\pi^{1}_{A,B+C};\psi_{B,C};\pi^{1}_{B,C})(c)

As such, for the left linear distributor, we get that:

\displaystyle F(\pi^{0}_{A,B+C})(a)\wedge(F(\pi^{1}_{A,B+C};\psi_{B,C};\pi^{0}_{B,C})(b)\vee F(\pi^{1}_{A,B+C};\psi_{B,C};\pi^{1}_{B,C})(c))

\displaystyle\leq F(\delta^{L}_{A,B,C})((F(\psi_{A\times B,C};\pi^{0}_{A\times B,C};\pi^{0}_{A,B})(a)\wedge F(\psi_{A\times B,C};\pi^{0}_{A\times B,C};\pi^{1}_{A,B})(b))

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\vee F(\psi_{A\times B,C};\pi^{1}_{A\times B,C})(c))

So
\delta^{L}
in
\int F
is well-defined, and similarly for the right linear distributor
\delta^{R}
. Since composition in
\int F
is the same as in
{\mathbb{B}}
, it follows that all the linear distributor axioms are satisfied. So we conclude that
\int F
is a CLDC, as desired.

\Box

We conclude this paper by applying this construction to the (double) power set functor on
\mathsf{REL}
, the category of sets and relations. Recall that
\mathsf{REL}
is a bicartesian category, with zero object being the empty set
\emptyset
and binary biproducts given by the disjoint union
\sqcup
.

Example 7.2

Let
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}\,\colon{\rm Rel}^{op}\rightarrow\mathbf{BDL}
be the contravariant powerset functor, mapping sets
X
to their powerset
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(X)
and mapping relations
R\,\colon X\rightarrow Y
to the “preimages”
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(R)\,\colon\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(Y)\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(X)
defined, for
V\subseteq Y
, to be
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(R)(V)=\{x\in X\,|\,\exists y\in V,xRy\}
. Then,
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}
has objects which are pairs of sets
(X,U)
with
U\subseteq X
and maps
R\,\colon(X,U)\rightarrow(Y,V)
which are relations
R\,\colon X\rightarrow Y
such that
U\subseteq\{x\in X\,|\,\exists y\in V,xRy\}
, in other words the relation
R
can restrict to a well-defined relation
R|_{U}\,\colon U\rightarrow V
. Now while
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}
is a CLDC, it turns out that its terminal object is also its initial object :
(\emptyset,\emptyset)
. As such,
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}
is an isomix CLDC, and thus by Thm
5.11
, it is a semi-additive category.

Example 7.3

In order to get a CLDC which is neither compact or posetal, we must instead consider the
double
powerset functor
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}\,\colon{\rm Rel}^{op}\rightarrow\mathbf{BDL}
, mapping sets
X
to the powerset of their powerset
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(X))
and mapping relations
R\,\colon X\rightarrow Y
to the “preimages of their images”
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}(R)\,\colon\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(Y))\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(X))
defined for
\mathbb{V}\subseteq\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(Y)
, to be
\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}(R)(\mathbb{V})=\{U\in\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(X)\,|\,R(U)\in\mathbb{V}\}
, where
R(U)=\{y\in Y,|\,\exists x\in U,xRy\}
. Then,
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}
has objects which are pairs of sets
(X,\mathbb{U})
with
\mathbb{U}\subseteq\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(X)
and maps
R\,\colon(X,\mathbb{U})\rightarrow(Y,\mathbb{Y})
that are relations
R\,\colon X\rightarrow Y
such that
\forall U\in\mathbb{U},R(U)\in\mathbb{V}
. Now
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}
is a CLDC which is neither compact nor posetal. Clearly,
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}
is not posetal, and hence not a bounded distributive lattice category. On the other hand, its terminal object is
(\emptyset,\{\emptyset\})
, while its initial object is
(\emptyset,\emptyset)
, thus we see that
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}
is not isomix, and hence not a semi-additive category. The product and coproduct in
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}
are given as follows:

(X,\mathbb{U})\times(Y,\mathbb{V})=(X\sqcup Y,\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}(\pi^{0}_{X,Y})(\mathbb{U})\cap\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}(\pi^{1}_{X,Y})(\mathbb{V}))=(X\sqcup Y,\mathbb{U}\times\mathbb{V})

(X,\mathbb{U})+(Y,\mathbb{V})=(X\sqcup Y,\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}(\pi^{0}_{X,Y})(\mathbb{U})\cup\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}(\pi^{1}_{X,Y})(\mathbb{V}))=(X\sqcup Y,(\mathbb{U}\times\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(Y))\cup(\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}(X)\times\mathbb{V}))

So
\int\mbox{${\mathcal{P}}$}^{2}
is a new interesting example of a CLDC, which is neither compact nor posetal.

Appendix A
Distributive Symmetric Monoidal Categories with Zero Objects

In this appendix we give full details of why the Kleisli category of the exception monad for a distributive category is an isomix SLDC. However, it turns out that this follows from a more general result about
distributive
symmetric monoidal categories with zero objects. Distributive symmetric monoidal categories are a generalization of distributive categories to a non-cartesian monoidal product, which describe the behaviour of the Kleisli category of the exception monad for a distributive category, since
\times
is a well-defined monoidal product on the Kleisli category, but not the binary product.

So recall that a symmetric monoidal category
(\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$},\oslash,I)
is
distributive

[
14
, Def 2.3.1]
if
{\mathbb{X}}
has finite coproducts and the following canonical transformations

d^{L}_{A,B,C}=[1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C},1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B,C}]\,\colon(A\oslash B)+(A\oslash C)\rightarrow A\oslash(B+C)

d^{R}_{A,B,C}=[\iota^{0}_{A,B}\oslash 1_{C},\iota^{1}_{A,B}\oslash 1_{C}]\,\colon(A\oslash C)+(B\oslash C)\rightarrow(A+B)\oslash C

u^{L}_{A}=b_{\bot\oslash A}\,\colon\bot\rightarrow\bot\oslash A\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988ptu^{R}_{A}=t_{A\oslash\bot}\,\colon\bot\rightarrow A\oslash\bot

are isomorphisms. There is a well-known folklore result that given such categories, we can build another symmetric monoidal product, which behaves as an “either-or-both” product. Indeed, given a distributive symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbb{X}}
, we get a functor
\curlyvee=(\cdot+\cdot)+(\cdot\oslash\cdot)\,\colon\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\times\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}\rightarrow\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$}
. We also get the following natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle{u^{R}_{\curlyvee}}_{A}={u^{R}_{+}}^{-1}_{A};{u^{R}_{+}}^{-1}_{A+\bot};(1_{A+\bot}+u^{R}_{A})=\iota^{0}_{A,\bot};\iota^{0}_{A+\bot,A\oslash\bot}\,\colon A\rightarrow A\curlyvee\bot

\displaystyle{u^{L}_{\curlyvee}}_{A}={u^{L}_{+}}^{-1}_{A};{u^{R}_{+}}^{-1}_{\bot+A};(1_{\bot+A}+u^{L}_{A})=\iota^{1}_{\bot,A};\iota^{0}_{\bot+A,\bot\oslash A}\,\colon A\rightarrow\bot\curlyvee A

\displaystyle{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B}={\sigma_{+}}_{A,B}+{\sigma_{\oslash}}_{A,B}\,\colon A\curlyvee B\rightarrow B\curlyvee A

\displaystyle{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}\,\colon(A\curlyvee B)\curlyvee C\rightarrow A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

where the last one is defined as the unique maps induced by the following maps:

\displaystyle A\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

\displaystyle B\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{B,C}}B+C\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}B\curlyvee C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

\displaystyle A\oslash B\xrightarrow{1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C}}A\oslash(B+C)\xrightarrow{1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

\displaystyle C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{B,C}}B+C\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}B\curlyvee C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

\displaystyle((A+B)+(A\oslash B))\oslash C\xrightarrow{{d^{R}_{A+B,A\oslash B,C}}^{-1}}((A+B)\oslash C)+((A\oslash B)\oslash C)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\xrightarrow{{d^{R}_{A,B,C}}^{-1}+1_{(A\oslash B)\oslash C}}((A\oslash C)+(B\oslash C))+((A\oslash B)\oslash C)\xrightarrow{\chi_{A,B,C}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

where
\chi_{A,B,C}
is given as the unique morphisms induced by the following maps

\displaystyle A\oslash C\xrightarrow{1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B,C}}A\oslash(B+C)\xrightarrow{1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

\displaystyle B\oslash C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}B\curlyvee C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

\displaystyle(A\oslash B)\oslash C\xrightarrow{{\alpha_{\oslash}}_{A,B,C}}A\oslash(B\oslash C)\xrightarrow{1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)}}A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C)

Lemma A.1

For a distributive symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbb{X}}
,
(\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$},\curlyvee,\bot)
is a symmetric monoidal category.

We know show that for a distributive symmetric monoidal category whose initial object is in fact a zero object, then it is an isomix SLDC. This in particular captures the Kleisli category of an exception monad.

Theorem A.2

Let
{\mathbb{X}}
be a distributive symmetric monoidal category with a zero object
{\bf 0}
. Then
{\mathbb{X}}
is an isomix SLDC, with tensor structure
(\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$},\curlyvee,{\bf 0})
and par structure
(\mbox{${\mathbb{X}}$},+,{\bf 0})
, where the left linear distributor
\llbracket\delta^{L}_{A,B,C}\rrbracket\,\colon A\curlyvee(B+C)\rightarrow((A\curlyvee B)+C)+\top
, which is explicitly of type:

\displaystyle(A+(B+C))+(A\oslash(B+C))\rightarrow((A+B)+(A\oslash B))+C

is defined as the unique map induced by the following maps:

A\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A,B}}A+B\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B}}A\curlyvee B\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C}}(A\curlyvee B)+C

B\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A,B}}A+B\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B}}A\curlyvee B\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C}}(A\curlyvee B)+C

C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C}}(A\curlyvee B)+C

A\oslash(B+C)\xrightarrow{1_{A}\oslash(1_{B}+t_{C})}A\oslash(B+{\bf 0})\xrightarrow{1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B}}A\oslash B\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B}}A\curlyvee B\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C}}(A\curlyvee B)+C

and the right linear distributor
\delta^{R}_{A,B,C}\,\colon(A+B)\curlyvee C\rightarrow A+(B\curlyvee C)
, which is explicitly of type:

\displaystyle((A+B)+C)+((A+B)\oslash C)\rightarrow A+((B+C)+(B\oslash C))

is defined as the unique map induced by the following maps

A\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)

B\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{B,C}}B+C\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}B\curlyvee C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)

C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{B,C}}B+C\xrightarrow{\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}B\curlyvee C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)

(A+B)\oslash C\xrightarrow{(t_{A}+1_{B})\oslash 1_{C}}({\bf 0}+B)\oslash C\xrightarrow{{{u^{L}_{+}}_{B}}\oslash 1_{C}}B\oslash C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C}}B\curlyvee C\xrightarrow{\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}}A+(B\curlyvee C)

Proof:

First, we show the left linear distributor is a natural transformation. Consider a triple of maps
f\,\colon A\rightarrow A^{\prime},g\,\colon B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
and
h\,\colon C\rightarrow C^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{X}}
. Then

\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)=(f\curlyvee(g+h));\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

holds by considering the left-hand side and right hand-side pre-composed with the relevant injections as follows.

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,B+C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=f;\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{1}_{A,B+C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=g;\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{1}_{A,B+C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=h;\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};\delta^{L}_{A,B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B}+t_{C});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};((f\curlyvee g)+h)

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B}+t_{C});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});(f\oslash g);\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(f\oslash(g+t_{C});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B^{\prime}});\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,B+C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};(f\curlyvee(g+h));\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=f;\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}+C^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime}),A^{\prime}\oslash(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime})};\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=f;\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{1}_{A,B+C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};(f\curlyvee(g+h));\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=g;\iota^{0}_{B^{\prime},C^{\prime}};\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}+C^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime}),A^{\prime}\oslash(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime})};\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=g;\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{1}_{A,B+C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};(f\curlyvee(g+h));\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=h;\iota^{1}_{B^{\prime},C^{\prime}};\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}+C^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime}),A^{\prime}\oslash(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime})};\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=h;\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A+(B+C),A\oslash(B+C)};(f\curlyvee(g+h));\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(f\oslash(g+h));\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime}+(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime}),A^{\prime}\oslash(B^{\prime}+C^{\prime})};\delta^{L}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(f\oslash(g+h));(1_{A^{\prime}}\oslash(1_{B^{\prime}}+t_{C^{\prime}});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B^{\prime}});\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(f\oslash(g+t_{C});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B^{\prime}});\iota^{1}_{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime},A^{\prime}\oslash B^{\prime}};\iota^{0}_{A^{\prime}\curlyvee B^{\prime},C^{\prime}}

Then, by the universal properties of coproducts, the left-hand side composite and right-hand side composite are equal, meaning
\delta^{L}
is natural.

Second, we show that the right linear distributor is natural and that this construction will be a symmetric LDC at the same time, by proving (
7
) holds, i.e.

\delta^{R}_{A,B,C}={\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

We consider the right-hand side pre-composed with the appropriate injections.

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A+B)+C,(A+B)\oslash C};{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{1}_{C,A+B};\iota^{0}_{C+(A+B),C\oslash(A+B)};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B};{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B};\iota^{1}_{C,B+A};\iota^{0}_{C+(B+A),C\oslash(B+A)};\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B,A};\iota^{1}_{C,B+A};\iota^{0}_{C+(B+A),C\oslash(B+A)};\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{C\curlyvee B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B\curlyvee C,A};{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B\curlyvee C}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A+B)+C,(A+B)\oslash C};{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{1}_{C,A+B};\iota^{0}_{C+(A+B),C\oslash(A+B)};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A,B};{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B};\iota^{1}_{C,B+A};\iota^{0}_{C+(B+A),C\oslash(B+A)};\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{C,B};\iota^{1}_{C,B+A};\iota^{0}_{C+(B+A),C\oslash(B+A)};\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{C,B};\iota^{0}_{C+B,C\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{C\curlyvee B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{C,B};\iota^{0}_{C+B,C\oslash B};{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,A};{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A+B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A+B)+C,(A+B)\oslash C};{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{C,A+B};\iota^{0}_{C+(A+B),C\oslash(A+B)};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{C,B};\iota^{0}_{C+B,C\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{C\curlyvee B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{C,B};\iota^{0}_{C+B,C\oslash B};{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,A};{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{(A+B)+C,(A+B)\oslash C};{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle={\sigma_{\oslash}}_{A+B,C};\iota^{1}_{C+(A+B),C\oslash(A+B)};(1_{C}\curlyvee{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle={\sigma_{\oslash}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\oslash{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});\iota^{1}_{C+(B+A),C\oslash(B+A)};\delta^{L}_{C,B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle={\sigma_{\oslash}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\oslash{\sigma_{+}}_{A,B});(1_{C}\oslash(1_{B}+t_{A});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{C+B,C\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{C\curlyvee B,A};({\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B}+1_{A});{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle={\sigma_{\oslash}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\oslash(t_{A}+1_{B});{\sigma_{+}}_{\top,B};{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{C+B,C\oslash B};{\sigma_{\curlyvee}}_{C,B};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,A};{\sigma_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C,A}

\displaystyle={\sigma_{\oslash}}_{A+B,C};(1_{C}\oslash(t_{A}+1_{B});{u^{L}_{+}}_{B});{\sigma_{\oslash}}_{C,B};\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}

\displaystyle=((t_{A}+1_{B});{u^{L}_{+}}_{B}\oslash 1_{C});{\sigma_{\oslash}}_{B,C};{\sigma_{\oslash}}_{C,B};\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}

\displaystyle=((t_{A}+1_{B});{u^{L}_{+}}_{B}\oslash 1_{C});\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C}

It remains now to show the coherence conditions between units and linear distributivities (
4
), associativities and linear distributivities (
5
), and left and right linear distributivities (
6
).

The first condition from (
4
) is
{u^{L}_{\curlyvee}}_{A+B};\delta^{L}_{{\bf 0},A,B}={u^{L}_{\curlyvee}}_{A}+1_{B}
and holds as follows.

\displaystyle{u^{L}_{\curlyvee}}_{A+B};\delta^{L}_{{\bf 0},A,B}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{{\bf 0},A+B};\iota^{0}_{{\bf 0}+(A+B),{\bf 0}\oslash(A+B)};\delta^{L}_{{\bf 0},A,B}

\displaystyle=[\iota^{1}_{{\bf 0},A};\iota^{0}_{{\bf 0}+A,{\bf 0}\oslash A};\iota^{0}_{{\bf 0}\curlyvee A,B},\iota^{1}_{{\bf 0}\curlyvee,B}]

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{{\bf 0},A};\iota^{0}_{{\bf 0}+A,{\bf 0}\oslash A}+1_{B}

\displaystyle={u^{L}_{\curlyvee}}_{A}+1_{B}

The second condition from (
4
) is
\delta^{L}_{A,B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}=1_{A}\curlyvee{u^{R}_{+}}_{A}
. It holds by the universal properties of coproducts and pre-composing with the relevant injections.

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,B+{\bf 0}};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+{\bf 0});A\curlyvee(B+{\bf 0})};\delta^{L}_{A,B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B}

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{B,{\bf 0}};\iota^{1}_{A,B+{\bf 0}};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+{\bf 0});A\curlyvee(B+{\bf 0})};\delta^{L}_{A,B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{B,{\bf 0}};\iota^{1}_{A,B+{\bf 0}};\iota^{0}_{A+(B+{\bf 0});A\curlyvee(B+{\bf 0})};\delta^{L}_{A,B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=b_{A\curlyvee C}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A+(B+{\bf 0});A\curlyvee(B+{\bf 0})};\delta^{L}_{A,B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B}+t_{\bf 0}));(1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B}

\displaystyle 1_{A}\curlyvee{u^{R}_{+}}_{B}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}+{u^{R}_{+}}_{B})+(1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B})

\displaystyle=[[\iota^{0}_{A,B},{u^{R}_{+}}_{B};\iota^{1}_{A,B}];\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B},(1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B}]

\displaystyle=[[\iota^{0}_{A,B},[1_{B},b_{B}];\iota^{1}_{A,B}];\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B},(1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B}]

\displaystyle=[[\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B},[\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B},b_{A\curlyvee C}]];,(1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B});\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B}]

\displaystyle=\delta^{L}_{A,B,{\bf 0}};{u^{R}_{+}}_{A\curlyvee B}

The third and fourth conditions from (
4
) then follow by the definition of
\delta^{R}
as a composite of braidings and
\delta^{L}
.

The first coherence condition from (
5
) is

\delta^{L}_{A\curlyvee B,C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})={\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

Once more, it holds by the universal properties of coproducts and pre-composing with the relevant injections. Firstly, the left-hand side:

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{A\curlyvee B,C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)\curlyvee C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{A\curlyvee B,C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{A\curlyvee B,C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)\curlyvee C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{A\curlyvee B,C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)\curlyvee C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{A\curlyvee B,C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{(A\curlyvee B)\curlyvee C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};\delta^{L}_{A\curlyvee B,C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=(1_{A\curlyvee B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D}));(1_{A\curlyvee B}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{1}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)\curlyvee C,D};({\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C}+1_{D})

\displaystyle=(1_{A\curlyvee B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D}));(1_{A\curlyvee B}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{1}_{(A\curlyvee B)+C,(A\curlyvee B)\oslash C};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A\curlyvee B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D}));(1_{A\curlyvee B}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});{d^{R}}^{-1}_{A+B,A\oslash B,C};({d^{R}}^{-1}_{A,B,D}+1_{(A\oslash B)\oslash C});\chi_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle={d^{R}}^{-1}_{A+B,A\oslash B,C+D};({d^{R}}^{-1}_{A,B,C+D}+1_{(A\oslash B)\oslash(C+D)});

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt(((1_{A}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C})+(1_{B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C}))+(1_{A\oslash B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C}));\chi_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

Secondly, the right-hand side:

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B\curlyvee(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A,B};\iota^{0}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{B,C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A+B,A\oslash B};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C+D});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{B,CD});\iota^{1}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,D});(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B\curlyvee C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,{\bf 0}});(1_{A}\oslash{u^{R}_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B,C});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{0}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{B,C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{C,D};\iota^{1}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{B,C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B\curlyvee C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{(A\curlyvee B)+(C+D),(A\curlyvee B)\oslash(C+D)};{\alpha_{\curlyvee}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle={d^{R}}^{-1}_{A+B,A\oslash B,C+D};({d^{R}}^{-1}_{A,B,C+D}+1_{(A\oslash B)\oslash(C+D)});\chi_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

It remains to show that

\displaystyle(((1_{A}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C})+(1_{B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C}))+(1_{A\oslash B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C}));\chi_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=\chi_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

which we show by pre-composing by injections once more.

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,\oslash(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{(A\oslash(C+D))+(B\oslash(C+D)),(A\oslash B)+(C+D)};{\small LHS}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{0}_{A\oslash C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{(A\oslash C)+(B\oslash C),(A\oslash B)+C};\chi_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B,C});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash[\iota^{0}_{C,{\bf 0}},t_{D};\iota^{1}_{C,{\bf 0}}];{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B,C});(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash[\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C},t_{D}]);\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A,\oslash(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{(A\oslash(C+D))+(B\oslash(C+D)),(A\oslash B)+(C+D)};{\small LHS}

\displaystyle=(1_{B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{1}_{A\oslash C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{(A\oslash C)+(B\oslash C),(A\oslash B)+C};\chi_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{(A\oslash(C+D))+(B\oslash(C+D)),(A\oslash B)+(C+D)};{\small LHS}

\displaystyle=(1_{A\oslash B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{1}_{(A\oslash C)+(B\oslash C),(A\oslash B)+C};\chi_{A,B,C};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A\oslash B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});{\alpha_{\oslash}}_{A,B,C};(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{0}_{A,\oslash(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{(A\oslash(C+D))+(B\oslash(C+D)),(A\oslash B)+(C+D)};{\small RHS}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B,C+D};\iota^{0}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\iota^{1}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash[\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,D},\iota^{1}_{B\curlyvee C,D}]);(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B\curlyvee C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A}\oslash[\iota^{1}_{B,C};\iota^{0}_{B+C,B\oslash C},t_{D}]);\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{A,\oslash(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{(A\oslash(C+D))+(B\oslash(C+D)),(A\oslash B)+(C+D)};{\small RHS}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee(C+D)};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=\iota^{1}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{B,C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=(1_{B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,D};\iota^{1}_{A,(B\curlyvee C)+D};\iota^{0}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle=(1_{B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{1}_{A,B\curlyvee C};\iota^{0}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle\iota^{1}_{(A\oslash(C+D))+(B\oslash(C+D)),(A\oslash B)+(C+D)};{\small RHS}

\displaystyle={\alpha_{\oslash}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee(C+D)),A\oslash(B\curlyvee(C+D))};(1_{A}\curlyvee\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle={\alpha_{\oslash}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B+(C+D),B\oslash(C+D)};\delta^{L}_{B,C,D});\iota^{1}_{A+((B\curlyvee C)+D),A\oslash((B\curlyvee C)+D)};\delta^{L}_{A,B\curlyvee C,D}

\displaystyle={\alpha_{\oslash}}_{A,B,C+D};(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C}));(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,D});

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B\curlyvee C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A\oslash B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});{\alpha_{\oslash}}_{A,B,C};(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C};\iota^{0}_{B\curlyvee C,D});

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt(1_{A}\oslash(1_{B\curlyvee C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{B\curlyvee C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

\displaystyle=(1_{A\oslash B}\oslash(1_{C}+t_{D});{u^{R}_{+}}_{C});{\alpha_{\oslash}}_{A,B,C};(1_{A}\oslash\iota^{1}_{B+C,B\oslash C});\iota^{1}_{A+(B\curlyvee C),A\oslash(B\curlyvee C)};\iota^{0}_{A\curlyvee(B\curlyvee C),D}

This completes the proof of the first coherence condition from (
5
).

The remaining coherence conditions of (
5
) and (
6
) hold by similar calculations (which we exclude simply because of their length and repetitive nature).
